# Security findings register

Prepared 2026-10-29 by security-engineer under `docs/security/severity-policy.md` (PO-12). Working tree at HEAD `cd3a49a`; local `origin/main` also points at `cd3a49a` (I did not fetch, so I cannot confirm the remote). Nothing in `src/` or `tests/` was changed to produce this file. This register does not clear any gate in `docs/RELEASE_GATES.md`.

How to read it:
- Severity is exactly one of CRITICAL / HIGH / MEDIUM / LOW. Reviews that used other wording were re-rated (stated in each group's note). Where unsure I took the higher level.
- Status: **OPEN**, **FIXED-VERIFIED** (I saw a test, a re-review result or the code), **FIXED-CONTRACT-ONLY** (added 2026-10-29: an independent re-review verified the fix in the contract text, schemas, policy file and contract tests, but the feature is unbuilt, so there is no runtime evidence; implementation tests are still required), **FIXED-UNVERIFIED** (a fix is claimed but I saw no evidence, or the claimed fix has not been re-reviewed), **DEFERRED-WITH-REASON**, **SUPERSEDED**.
- A finding is *resolved* only when it is FIXED-VERIFIED or SUPERSEDED. OPEN, FIXED-CONTRACT-ONLY, FIXED-UNVERIFIED and DEFERRED-WITH-REASON all still block their gate (a DEFERRED row stops blocking only if the Product Owner records a written downgrade; LOW rows block nothing).
- Gate: PUSH for CRITICAL/HIGH, PRODUCTION for MEDIUM, NONE for LOW.
- Evidence tags: **RAN 2026-10-29** means the test is part of the full .NET run I executed that day (see 'What I ran'). **READ** means I read the code or document. A named review means I am relying on that reviewer's recorded result.
- Paths in the Evidence column that start with `tests/` are under `src/api/`; paths starting `src/` or `docs/` are from the repository root.
- IDs: review-local IDs clash across reviews (B1-nn, N-nn), so the register prefixes them (M1, M1R, M2C, M2B1, M2B2, M2B3, M2B3R, P2C, P2R, P2D, P2B1, CV, CVR, GR, NEW). CVR is the calendar.v1 re-review (`docs/security/calendar-v1-contract-rereview.md`), whose new findings N-1..N-8 are CVR-N01..CVR-N08.
- Updated 2026-10-29 from the calendar.v1 re-review: CV-01 and CV-02 are FIXED-VERIFIED at contract level; CV-03..CV-24 are FIXED-CONTRACT-ONLY (or DEFERRED-WITH-REASON for CV-21); CVR-N01..N08 added as LOW. No other row was touched. GR-1 stays OPEN HIGH: only the Product Owner can downgrade it, and no ruling exists.

# Part I. Gate summary

## 1. Open CRITICAL

**None.** No review recorded a Critical finding, and I found none while confirming evidence (including the A004 migration, the renamed route, LanguageGuard and the web redesign).

## 2. Open HIGH (blocks git push): 1

The policy says no push, and no offer to push, while any HIGH is open. **None is unresolved now (PPR-01 closed 2026-11-04, PO session 2026-11-04):**

- **PPR-01** (pilot-plan-review, HIGH, status FIXED-VERIFIED since PO session 2026-11-04, gate PUSH, no longer blocking): the PO, not the fixer, ran (a)-(d) in the Claude prompt without `az login`; results `servicePrincipal`; one `servicePrincipal` line, no `user`; `AZURE_CONFIG_DIR` unset (confirmed in bash by my own check); one line `Reader`. Still in force: never `az login` as the PO in a normal terminal (Owner sign-in only in the portal, or with a separate `AZURE_CONFIG_DIR` then `az logout`); breaking it reopens PPR-01. Still open, not closure conditions: Foundry keys were never listed (Lead claim, UNVERIFIED; regenerate on any doubt and after Stage 1, PPR-06) and two-step sign-in (MFA) on the PO's Owner account (UNVERIFIED, Stage 1 precondition 1.2). History of the row: agent shells could reuse the PO's cached Owner Azure CLI sign-in on a subscription that bills since PO-20be. Claimed fix 2026-11-04 (Lead, PO-20bf): owner login ended, Reader-only identity `tether-reader` used by agents, a test write was refused with AuthorizationFailed. 2026-11-04 00:21 (local): the Lead, who also made the fix, ran (a)-(d) and reported the expected results (details in the row); that is a self-check, not independent verification. (My earlier wording here, 'the PO or Lead runs', was too loose: the Lead made the fix, so the Lead's run cannot close it.) **To clear:** the PO repeats (a)-(d) in the PO's own terminal, without running `az login` as the PO, and records the dated result (Review Desk or `docs/DECISIONS.md`, no ids or keys); I then move it to FIXED-VERIFIED. Alternatively the PO downgrades it in writing. Key regeneration (only if keys could have been printed) and MFA on the Owner account are open side items, not closure conditions.

Earlier, the only HIGH that was open, GR-1, was downgraded to MEDIUM by the Product Owner on 2026-10-29 (PO-14a, in writing, with the reason below). It remains a production blocker:

- **GR-1** (google-tokens GR-1, now MEDIUM, status OPEN): Transcript/study-guide copied into an institution-managed Google account becomes visible to the institution.
  - Why it is still open: PO-10j allows Workspace accounts with a warning and PO-10l makes transcript upload per-session opt-in, but legal review (G1) is open and the rating was 'High until PO/legal decide'. The Product Owner has not ruled. No code implements any Docs/Drive write. Only the Product Owner can downgrade (severity-policy.md).

**AI notes after Stop (4)** (mock provider only; the provider guard refuses any real vendor, so these block a real provider as well as production)

- NAS-04 [OPEN]: Grounding is now checked, but prompt injection can still produce a cited, stored note (residual and NAS-R01 bypasses); real-model eval, language-guard enforcement and the "AI-generated" label are not done (G2).
- NAS-05 [OPEN]: noticeVersion is required, allow-listed and stored on the press and the audit row (engineering part verified by reading); the notice text, pre-press confirmation and "sent to AI on <date>" are G1 and not done.
- NAS-R01 [OPEN]: The grounding check accepts meaning-changing edits: dropped negation, month or weekday swaps, number words, and Unicode number forms (circled, superscript, Roman).
- NAS-R02 [OPEN]: The 1,800 s queue-wait limit is applied only on a GET or press; the job starts a press of any age, so the transcript can be sent hours after the press.

**Authentication, sessions, cookies (4)**

- M1-F07 [OPEN]: __Host- cookie done; DataProtection keys not encrypted at rest, no compose volume, Production only warns when KeysPath is unset.
- M1-F08b [OPEN]: No audit events for login, logout, session revocation or ownership violations (SEC-028).
- M2C-08 [OPEN]: Session cookie SameSite: contract text says Strict, code is Lax; rationale (OAuth return) is documented only in the uncommitted API_CONTRACTS_CALENDAR.md.
- NEW-02 [DEFERRED-WITH-REASON]: SEC-005 lockout/back-off not built (vacuous until Google sign-in exists).

**Edge: proxy, headers, CSRF, rate limits (1)**

- M1-F04 [OPEN]: Origin-only CSRF has no second layer (Sec-Fetch-Site or custom header). The WebSocket/hub Origin gap is closed.

**Logging (1)**

- B1V-01 [OPEN]: Provider-scoped logging config (Logging:Console:LogLevel:Default and similar) overrides the code filters, so the categories that carry student text can be switched back on. Reproduced in a scratch copy: 9 sentinel-bearing records. 2026-11-04 (pilot-plan-review section 5): a **Stage 1 precondition** for any real provider, and the fix must also cover the adapters' new log categories (HttpClient, Azure.Core, the Speech SDK's own log file) and their error paths.

**Web app (2)**

- M2C-16 [FIXED-UNVERIFIED]: Recording indicator must reflect server state AND the local mic track.
- M2C-29 [OPEN]: Web app must send CSP connect-src and Permissions-Policy (microphone=(self)); test that no provider key is in the bundle.

**Syllabus (6 open: P2C-23, SB-01, SBV-19, SBV-20, SBV-21, SBV-22; SBV-05, SBV-10, SBV-11, SBV-12, SBV-14, SBV-18, SBV-32, SBV-35 and SBV-37 are listed for history and are FIXED-VERIFIED)**

- P2C-23 [FIXED-UNVERIFIED]: Syllabus load could starve live-lecture AI budget (per-pool floor).
- SB-01 [OPEN]: A CHECK violation on a text-bearing syllabus row puts 'Failing row contains (...)' (snippets, titles, possibly the whole canonical text) in the PostgreSQL server log, outside the app log filters and outside delete. Fix: log_error_verbosity=terse in every environment plus a DB-log canary. Re-review 2026-10-31: compose settings and canary script authored (READ, correct); never run in CI; production parameter group and non-superuser app role outstanding; residuals SBR-04, SBR-05. 2026-11-01: the dev-compose canary now runs and passes in GitHub CI (run 41336514945, api and four e2e jobs, success); PO-20p makes the production DB log hardening a deployment checklist item with the hosting decision. Still OPEN.
- SBV-05 [FIXED-VERIFIED 2026-11-01, READ, NOT RUN; commit 400f1cc; `sbr-fixes-verification.md` "Third pass"] (added 2026-11-01; PO-20b 2026-11-01: build the list on Review, owner frontend-engineer): Unclaimed dates ("Dates we noticed but did not turn into items") are stored but no web component fetches or renders them, and the dropped notice shows only when droppedInvalid > 0. A date on a dated line without a recognised keyword, or a second date on a claimed line, is therefore shown nowhere and nothing hints that it exists (reachable with the mock and ordinary syllabi; a real extractor could be steered to put a deadline there). Fix: render the list on Review, or a count-free note when counts.unclaimedDates > 0 (PO scope ruling, UX open question 2). Closes SBR-06 too.
- SBV-10 [FIXED-VERIFIED 2026-11-02, fifth pass, commit 6618450; READ, syllabus unit tests RAN 236 passed, integration NOT RUN] (added 2026-11-02, fourth pass): item flags and confidence (notably `supersession_cue`) were stored as the extractor wrote them. The server now re-derives the context-based flags from the stored quote and server context, unions them with the extractor's and only lowers confidence. Residual preconditions for a real extractor: SBV-12, SBV-13, SBV-14, SBV-15, SBV-16.
- SBV-11 [FIXED-VERIFIED 2026-11-02, sixth pass, commit bb9b124; READ, syllabus unit tests RAN 260 passed and shown failing on the parent commit's source, integration NOT RUN; residual SBV-17 LOW: the DB check still covers resolved items only, no backfill; SBV-17 FIXED-VERIFIED 2026-11-02, seventh pass, 689a544, migration S003] (added 2026-11-02, fifth pass; reachable with the mock today, pre-existing, not caused by the SBV-10 change): a policy item (`not_applicable`) whose line has a supersession cue ('unless', 'except', 'is not', 'no longer', 'changed', 'updated'...) or that sits in a document with an injection marker or low text quality is stored with that flag and `probably_right`. syllabus-item.schema.json (allOf, P2-08/P2-19) requires `please_check` for those three flags on ANY item; the DB check covers resolved items only, so the row is accepted and served, and the web `parseItem` rejects it, which makes the whole item page unusable (`parsePage`: 'one bad row makes the page unusable'). An ordinary late-work policy line can stop the student reviewing that syllabus at all; it fails closed (no wrong date shown). Fix: the shared confidence rule gives please_check for a not_applicable item with any of the three flags (or the contract stops attaching them to policy items; Lead owns the text); widen the DB check to match; a test that pastes 'Late work policy: not accepted unless approved' and schema-validates every served item. Owners backend-engineer, Lead Architect (contract).
- SBV-12 [FIXED-VERIFIED 2026-11-02, eighth pass, commit 347296b; READ, syllabus unit tests RAN 411 passed, scratch probe RAN, integration NOT RUN] (added 2026-11-02, fifth pass; precondition for any real extractor, not reachable with the mock): the server did not check that the resolved date is a date written in the quote, that `explicit_full` has a written year, that an ambiguous numeric date ('3/4') was not resolved, or that a single date is not dressed as a recurrence. `ServerDates.Consistent` now drops (counted, quote kept as an unclaimed date) any item whose date is not read from a date whole inside the server-cut quote. Residuals: SBV-18..SBV-22 (MEDIUM) and SBV-23..SBV-31 (LOW).
- SBV-18 [FIXED-VERIFIED 2026-11-02, ninth pass, commit ad6bee5; READ, syllabus unit tests RAN 475 passed and the new tests shown failing on the parent commit's source (48 of 64), scratch probes RAN, integration NOT RUN; residuals SBV-33 and SBV-34 (LOW) and the time misread SBV-32 (MEDIUM, pre-existing)] (added 2026-11-02, eighth pass; **reachable with the mock today**): the shared date matcher reads things that are not a day as a day. 'Sept 2026' reads as Sep 20 and '5 October 2026' as Oct 20 (the day group takes the first two digits of the year); 'Dec 123' reads as Dec 12, 'may 3' (the verb) as May 3, 'problems 9/30' and 'worth 5/20 points' as dates. The mock stores 'Paper due by Sept 2026' as Sep 20, 2026 and 'Quiz on 5 October 2026' as Oct 20, 2026, both resolved `probably_right` ('The year comes from your term dates.'), and the SBV-12 check accepts the same reading from a real extractor. Not batch-approvable (batch needs looks_right) and the quote is shown, but it is a wrong deadline presented as probably right. Owners backend-engineer, ai-agent-engineer.
- SBV-19 [OPEN] (added 2026-11-02, eighth pass; real-extractor precondition): rule (b) of SBV-12 (explicit_full needs a written year) is applied to single dates only. A recurrence, which by rule (d) has no written date at all, is kept with `derivation = explicit_full` and then gets `looks_right`, the reason 'The full date is written in your syllabus.' (false) and batch approval. Owners backend-engineer, ai-agent-engineer.
- SBV-20 [OPEN] (added 2026-11-02, eighth pass; real-extractor precondition): the recurrence check accepts any weekday-like token in the quote, of any item kind, with no time required: 'Students who sat the midterm' (SA), 'Molar mass MW' (MO, WE) and 'No class Mondays' (MO, no supersession cue fires) all keep a weekly rule, `probably_right`. Owners backend-engineer, ai-agent-engineer.
- SBV-21 [OPEN] (added 2026-11-02, eighth pass; design doc condition 5 'Still not covered'; real-extractor precondition): the `alternatives` of a conflicting item are not checked against the quote, and the web shows them as the dates the item 'could be' (ItemRow.tsx:163), so a model can offer a fabricated date for the student to pick. Owners backend-engineer, ai-agent-engineer.
- SBV-22 [OPEN] (added 2026-11-02, eighth pass; condition 5 'Still not covered'; real-extractor precondition): the time, end time and time zone of a resolved item are not checked against the quote, so a verbatim 'Final exam: Dec 9, 2026 at 2:00 PM' can be stored at 9:00 AM with `looks_right`. Tenth pass (19796cd): still OPEN; the mock's own time misreads (SBV-35, SBV-36) are stored unchecked too, and a server time rule must match the mock's new reading (first clock after the date, bounded at the next date). Eleventh pass (c729753): still OPEN; the rule must now also follow the mock's colon rule (no am/pm: hour 13-23 or a leading zero), the glued-zone rule, and must not copy the SBV-37 cross-noon misread. Owners backend-engineer, ai-agent-engineer.
- SBV-37 [FIXED-VERIFIED 2026-11-02, twelfth pass, commit 9684247; READ, syllabus unit tests RAN 573 passed and the SBV37 tests shown failing on the parent's source (16 of 26, controls pass), scratch probe RAN, integration NOT RUN; when only the end has am/pm and the start would be at or after the end, the start takes the other half of the day, else no time; residuals SBV-39 and SBV-40 (LOW); a server time rule (SBV-22) must follow the same reading] (added 2026-11-02, eleventh pass; **reachable with the mock today**; pre-existing, not caused by c729753): in a time range with the meridiem written only on the end, the end's meridiem is applied to the start, so a range across noon becomes an evening start: 'Final exam Dec 9 11:00-1:00 PM' and '11-1pm' are stored at 23:00 (the end is dropped because 13:00 < 23:00), '10-12pm' at 22:00, '10:30-12:30 PM' at 22:30, '11:30-12:45pm' at 23:30; weekly 'Office hours Tuesdays 11-1pm' is 23:00. `probably_right` (with a written year `looks_right`, batch-approvable). A confidently wrong exam time 12 hours late from an ordinary layout, so it stays OPEN despite the stop-polishing decision. Unsure between LOW and MEDIUM, so MEDIUM (PO-15/16: blocks production, not a push). Fix: when only the end has a meridiem and the shared reading puts the start after the end, read the start in the other half of the day (11-1pm is 11:00-13:00), or read no time. Owners backend-engineer, Lead Architect.
- SBV-35 [FIXED-VERIFIED 2026-11-02, eleventh pass, commit c729753; READ, syllabus unit tests RAN 548 passed, scratch probe RAN, integration NOT RUN; the clock scan stops at the start of the next date hit] (added 2026-11-02, tenth pass; **reachable with the mock today**; pre-existing): a time written for a later date on the same line is given to the first date. 'Quiz Dec 9; Exam Dec 11 at 2pm' is stored Dec 9 at 14:00; with written years it is `looks_right` and batch-approvable. Sweep: 128 of 128. Unsure between LOW and MEDIUM, so MEDIUM (PO-15/16 calibration: blocks production, not a push). Fix: bound the clock scan at the next date hit. Owners backend-engineer, Lead Architect.
- SBV-32 [FIXED-VERIFIED 2026-11-02, tenth pass, commit 19796cd; READ, syllabus unit tests RAN 510 passed and HEAD's DateMatcherTests shown failing on the parent's source (26 of 99), scratch probe RAN, integration NOT RUN; the time is read only after the date and the meridiem needs a non-letter after it; residuals SBV-35 (MEDIUM) and SBV-36 (LOW)] (added 2026-11-02, ninth pass; **reachable with the mock today**; pre-existing, now asserted by the new SBV-30 tests): the mock's clock reader `FindTime` ignores its `afterIndex` argument, so the day of a date followed by a hyphen and a time is read as the start hour. 'Homework due Dec 9 - 1pm' is stored Dec 9 at 9:00 PM (8 hours after the written time), 'Homework due Dec 10 - 2:00 PM' at 10:00 PM, 'Paper due Dec 11 - 9am' at 11:00 AM; the meridiem has no word boundary, so 'Building 3 Amos Hall' is 3:00 AM. All `probably_right`. Unsure between LOW and MEDIUM (same harm as SBV-18, a deadline shown later than written), so MEDIUM. Fix: start the clock scan after the date hit, add a word boundary after am/pm, and have the Lead decide the expected readings in `DateMatcherTests` ('Dec 9 - 11pm' asserted as 9 to 11 PM). Owners backend-engineer, Lead Architect.
- SBV-14 [FIXED-VERIFIED 2026-11-02, sixth pass, commit bb9b124; READ, syllabus unit tests RAN 260 passed and shown failing on the parent commit's source, integration NOT RUN; the stored reason is always from a fixed 14-string vocabulary, none of it new copy] (added 2026-11-02, fifth pass; precondition for any real extractor): `confidenceReason` is the extractor's free text unless the server lowers the confidence. API_CONTRACTS_P2.md line 109 says confidence is server-derived and the reason is fixed vocabulary. The reason is the one line the web shows next to the item (ItemRow.tsx:168; flags are not rendered), so a steered model can put persuasive text ('the NOT is a typo, the date is confirmed') beside a please_check item at the moment the student acknowledges it. Rated MEDIUM (unsure between LOW and MEDIUM; the forcing itself holds). Fix: always set the reason from the server rule. Owners backend-engineer, ai-agent-engineer.

Still blocks production.

## 6. Check recipes for the Lead

### Before-push check (no open CRITICAL or HIGH)

Run from `<repo>`. Every step must pass; then say the result plainly.

1. See what would be pushed: `git status --short` and `git log origin/main..HEAD --oneline`. Untracked files are not pushed until added; the policy still applies to anything you add.
2. No unresolved CRITICAL or HIGH in the register tables (this must print nothing):
   ```
   grep -E '^\| [^|]+ \| [^|]+ \| (CRITICAL|HIGH) \| (OPEN|FIXED-CONTRACT-ONLY|FIXED-UNVERIFIED|DEFERRED-WITH-REASON) \|' docs/security/findings-register.md
   ```
   **As of 2026-11-04, after PPR-01 moved to FIXED-VERIFIED (PO session 2026-11-04), it prints nothing again.** Still nothing after the 2026-11-05 Demo Doc export design review (no CRITICAL or HIGH added). Earlier the same day it printed one row, PPR-01 (HIGH, FIXED-UNVERIFIED), and blocked a push. Before that it printed nothing: the Product Owner downgraded GR-1 from HIGH to MEDIUM on 2026-10-29 (PO-14a). Check the legal register too (`docs/legal/legal-register.md`); both must print nothing before a push.
3. No review newer than the register has been left out of it. This must print nothing, or each file it lists must be reflected in the register first:
   ```
   find docs/security -name '*.md' -newer docs/security/findings-register.md ! -name findings-register.md
   ```
4. Any review written after this register uses the same four levels; add its rows to Part II before the check in step 2 is trusted.
5. Backend and contracts still green (5 projects, one command; Docker must be running):
   ```
   docker compose -f docker-compose.yml -f docker-compose.dev.yml run --rm dotnet dotnet test src/api/Tether.slnx
   ```
   Web (`npm test`, typecheck, lint in `src/web`) is a separate step I did not run.
6. Nothing secret in the tree being pushed (hits in `*.cs` test fixtures such as `sk-ant-test` are expected; anything else is a finding):
   ```
   git grep -nI -E 'sk-ant-[A-Za-z0-9]|AKIA[0-9A-Z]{12}|BEGIN (RSA|EC|OPENSSH|PRIVATE) KEY|AIza[0-9A-Za-z_-]{30}|client_secret|refresh_token *[:=]' -- . ':!docs' ':!*.md'; git ls-files --others --exclude-standard | grep -i -E '\.env|\.pem|\.key|secret'
   ```
7. Record the result in the next progress note: 'Register checked at <HEAD>: open CRITICAL n, open HIGH n, push allowed / not allowed'.

### Before-production check (no open CRITICAL, HIGH or MEDIUM)

   ```
   grep -E '^\| [^|]+ \| [^|]+ \| (CRITICAL|HIGH|MEDIUM) \| (OPEN|FIXED-CONTRACT-ONLY|FIXED-UNVERIFIED|DEFERRED-WITH-REASON) \|' docs/security/findings-register.md
   ```

Earlier on 2026-11-05 (Demo Doc export design review) it printed 50 rows (0 HIGH + 50 MEDIUM: the 48 below plus DXD-01 and DXD-02), and the MEDIUM-only count below prints 50. As of 2026-11-04 (after the PPR-01 closure) it printed 48 rows (0 HIGH + 48 MEDIUM: the 39 described next plus the 9 pilot plan rows PPR-02..PPR-09 and PPR-11), and the MEDIUM-only count below prints 48; earlier that day it printed 49 (PPR-01 HIGH included). Before 2026-11-04 it printed 39 rows (0 HIGH + 39 MEDIUM, of which 11 are calendar.v1 rows FIXED-CONTRACT-ONLY, 4 are notes-after-Stop rows NAS-04, NAS-05, NAS-R01, NAS-R02, 1 is the syllabus-as-built row SB-01, 4 are syllabus rows SBV-19..SBV-22 (eighth pass, real-extractor preconditions) (SBV-37 left this list on 2026-11-02, twelfth pass; SBV-35 left this list on 2026-11-02, eleventh pass; SBV-32 left this list on 2026-11-02, tenth pass; SBV-18 left it on 2026-11-02, ninth pass; SBV-12 left it on 2026-11-02, eighth pass; SBV-10 left it on 2026-11-02, fifth pass, and SBV-11 and SBV-14 on 2026-11-02, sixth pass, all FIXED-VERIFIED), 1 is the CI supply-chain row SBR-01 (DEFERRED-WITH-REASON, PO-20o), 1 is the public-repo row SEC-PUBLIC (DEFERRED-WITH-REASON, PO-20d), and GR-1 is one of the 39; the unclaimed-dates row SBV-05 left this list on 2026-11-01, third pass, FIXED-VERIFIED). Expected MEDIUM-only count: `grep -cE '^\| [^|]+ \| [^|]+ \| MEDIUM \| (OPEN|FIXED-CONTRACT-ONLY|FIXED-UNVERIFIED|DEFERRED-WITH-REASON) \|' docs/security/findings-register.md` prints 49 (50 earlier on 2026-11-05, 48 before 2026-11-05, 39 before 2026-11-04). Per-status counts for section 5: `for s in CRITICAL HIGH MEDIUM LOW; do for st in FIXED-VERIFIED FIXED-CONTRACT-ONLY FIXED-UNVERIFIED OPEN DEFERRED-WITH-REASON SUPERSEDED; do echo "$s $st $(grep -cE "^\| [^|]+ \| [^|]+ \| $s \| $st \|" docs/security/findings-register.md)"; done; done`.
2. For every FIXED-VERIFIED row you rely on, re-open the evidence (test name still exists and passes in CI, not only locally). `grep -c 'RAN 2026-10-29' docs/security/findings-register.md` shows how many rest on my local run; none rests on a GitHub CI run because CI has never run (NEW-03).
3. Controls that exist only as contract text or design and have no implementation yet. They are not open rows today because the code does not exist, but each becomes a production blocker the moment its feature ships. Confirm none of these features is in the release, or implement and test them first:
   - Syllabus parser sandbox, ingest worker, tmpfs volume, hostile-file fixtures (P2C-01, P2C-02, P2D-R3-03..06; contract 5.1).
   - Real STT vendor controls: daily STT-minutes budget, global kill switch, vendor retention terms (M2C-25, G2).
   - Real Claude adapter: PII redaction decision (M2B3-12 / B3-12), disclosure that an in-flight request cannot be recalled, regex/prompt Cf-character handling (M2B3R-N07), per-user quick-recap limit (M2B3-05), shared-state backplane (single-instance only, ADR-020d).
   - Google: token cipher and KEK custody, OAuth client and verification, Calendar and Docs adapters, quotas (GR-1..GR-7). calendar.v1 is closed only at contract level (CV-01..CV-24, re-review 2026-10-29): before the Calendar module ships, its section 16 'Required' tests must exist and pass, in particular CV-01/CV-02 (extended delete scan, disconnect exits, account deletion with Google failing), CV-03 (fake-Google drift, `If-Match`, normalisation), CV-04 (two-user mismatch), CV-05 (mass `invalid_client` deletes nothing; table-driven match to `calendar-policy.json`, CVR-N06), CV-06/CV-13 (load tests; PO/devops approve the PROPOSAL numbers), CV-07 (withdraw during run), CV-08 (plan-changed zero writes per kind), CV-10/CV-11, CV-12 (cross-user digest replay), the log canary (CV-15), and Google behaviour tests T1-T12 (NOT RUN).
   - Deployment prerequisites added by batch 1 (no Dockerfile or deployment definition exists yet; none of this is enforced by an artifact): `Security__RootSecret` (base64, 32+ bytes, from the secret store; startup refuses it otherwise), `AllowedHosts` (public host names, not `*`), a runtime image with ICU (libicu) and tzdata, `Security__TrustedProxies`/`TrustedProxyNetworks` only for the real proxy. Wrong values stop startup (tested), but B1V-01 and B1V-05 must be closed first. Added 2026-11-01 (PO-20p, SB-01): repeat the PostgreSQL log hardening (`log_error_verbosity=terse`, `log_parameter_max_length_on_error=0`, `log_statement=none`, non-superuser app role) on the production database with the hosting decision, and run the DB log canary against it. Added 2026-11-01 (PO-20d, SEC-PUBLIC): decide repository visibility before any real deployment or real data.
   - Account deletion and evidence retention (NEW-01), sign-in lockout (NEW-02), multi-instance revocation.
4. Scanning and operations (gate G5): CodeQL, dependency, secret and container scan results from a real CI run; a deployed telemetry sample; a backup restore drill including a KEK restore (NEW-03).

## 7. New findings from the rename, redesign and language-guide work

Examined: migration `A004_RenameLostTrackToQuickRecap`, the renamed route and its limits, `LanguageGuard` and its log output, `public/prefs-init.js`, the new web shell, command palette and Quick recap panel.

**No new CRITICAL, HIGH or MEDIUM finding.** What I checked and what I saw:
- **A004 migration.** It drops the CHECK, relabels `ai.ai_usage.workflow` and three `prompt_version` columns, and re-adds the CHECK. The ledger amounts and reservation rows are untouched. There are no triggers on the `ai` tables, so the UPDATE is legal. `AiScopeMigrationTests` exercises Up, Down and Up again against legacy rows and passed in my run. Side effect recorded as RN-01 (stored provenance labels are rewritten; accepted by PO-11a).
- **Renamed route.** `POST /api/v1/sessions/{id}/quick-recap` is registered in the same group as before (`AiEndpoints.cs:24`). Authentication, the Origin check and the global limiters are applied by the host to every `/api/v1` route; the per-session recap limiter and Idempotency-Key logic live in `CatchUpService`, not under a route name. No exemption or bucket in `Tether.Host/Program.cs` or `SeparateRateBucket` mentions the old or new name (grep). The web client calls the new path (`lib/api/client.ts:131`); no alias to the old path remains, so the old URL is a 404. `AiRouteTests` (RAN, pass) cover 401, cross-user 404, Origin, content type, idempotency and rate limit on the new route (RN-02).
- **LanguageGuard log canary.** Logs carry rule ids and counts only; the canary test passes (RN-03). Gaps are Info only: it uses a fake logger, default mode is log-only, and quoted transcript text exempts wording (RN-03..RN-05).
- **Web redesign.** No `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`, `innerHTML` or `eval` in non-test web source (grep); `prefs-init.js` sets only whitelisted attributes; localStorage holds preferences and the Quick recap window, not transcript text. The web app still sends no CSP or Permissions-Policy, which is the existing open MEDIUM M2C-29, now with one more same-origin script to allow (RN-06).
- Open items touching the uncommitted code were already in the register (M2C-29, M2B3-05).

This also shows the uncommitted rename, A004 and LanguageGuard code builds and its tests pass. No test, build, workflow, canary script or app was run (the dev API and a dev server were in use). Commit figures (131 syllabus, 600 integration x3, 2967 web unit, and so on) are the authors' claims. ## Google token and data-residency design (google-tokens-and-data-residency.md, GR-1..GR-7)

Reviewer ratings were: GR-1 'High until PO/legal decide', GR-2 Medium, GR-3 Medium, GR-4 Low, GR-5 Medium, GR-6 Medium, GR-7 'blocking for real use' (rated MEDIUM here). Design only; no token code, Docs adapter or Google call exists.

| ID | Source | Sev | Status | Gate | Summary | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| GR-1 | google-tokens GR-1 | MEDIUM | OPEN | PRODUCTION | Transcript/study-guide copied into an institution-managed Google account becomes visible to the institution. | PO-10j allows Workspace accounts with a warning and PO-10l makes transcript upload per-session opt-in, but legal review (G1) is open and the rating was 'High until PO/legal decide'. No code implements any Docs/Drive write. DOWNGRADED HIGH to MEDIUM by the Product Owner on 2026-10-29 (PO-14a): school-managed Google accounts are out of scope (PO-14), so the institution-visibility exposure does not exist for supported accounts. Reason it stays MEDIUM, not closed: the app must detect an organisation account (`hd` claim, presence with only `openid` scopes UNVERIFIED) and refuse to connect it; that enforcement is not built and must be tested before production or any real Google connection. |
| GR-2 | google-tokens GR-2 | MEDIUM | FIXED-UNVERIFIED | PRODUCTION | Deleting in Tether does not remove Google copies; users may assume it does. | PO-10m: dialogs must say so; calendar.v1 5.4 claims it. Docs delete UX not designed. Unreviewed. |
| GR-3 | google-tokens GR-3 | MEDIUM | DEFERRED-WITH-REASON | PRODUCTION | Token compromise on a compromised host reaches only Tether-created files/calendar. | ITokenCipher, KEK custody and scope allowlist test are not built (token doc section 5 items 3-4, 13). |
| GR-4 | google-tokens GR-4 | LOW | DEFERRED-WITH-REASON | NONE | KEK loss forces mass reconnect. | Rotation/restore drill not built. |
| GR-5 | google-tokens GR-5 | MEDIUM | DEFERRED-WITH-REASON | PRODUCTION | drive.file capability assumptions wrong, forcing a broader scope. | Vendor tests T1-T12 NOT RUN (calendar contract CV-28). |
| GR-6 | google-tokens GR-6 | MEDIUM | DEFERRED-WITH-REASON | PRODUCTION | Student shares a Doc containing third-party voices or weakness notes. | Student-controlled; warning copy not written (neurodivergent-ux/legal). |
| GR-7 | google-tokens GR-7 | MEDIUM | DEFERRED-WITH-REASON | PRODUCTION | Unverified OAuth app: test users only, 7-day refresh expiry; unusable for real students. | Blocking for real use; Google Cloud project/OAuth client not created (PO-9g). |

READ RootSecret.cs:90-96. |
| B1V-05 | batch 1 verification | LOW | OPEN | NONE | No root-secret rotation procedure: no RootSecretPrevious, IdempotencyHmacKeyPrevious takes a raw key (no tool prints the derived one), no previous ring for the audit HMAC. ## AI notes after Stop (notes-after-stop-review.md, commit 0a60d51; notes-after-stop-rereview.md, commit afde659)

PO-18d (option B): a deliberate "Make notes" press after Stop is the only thing that sends a stopped lecture's transcript to the AI. All rows are on the mock provider; the provider guard refuses any real vendor (`AiOptions.cs:186-194`). Evidence tag READ = code and test read on 2026-10-31, NOT RUN. Paths are under `src/api/`.

Decisions that touch these findings: **PO-18c** (Demo-source notice shown as a one-line note with Details, wording unchanged) is a Product Owner risk acceptance for the personal-use scope; counsel was not consulted and **G1 is not cleared** by it; the less prominent notice must be included in the G1 notice review alongside NAS-05. **PO-18a** (no decoration on the consent screen in non-Paper themes, consent card unchanged) and **PO-18b** (syllabus reserved-space rail) change no security finding. **PO-18d** is the basis of every row below.

| SBV-01 | sbr-fixes-verification | LOW | FIXED-VERIFIED | NONE | The 'verbatim, server-sliced snippet' invariant that the SBR-06 demotion relies on is stated but not enforced: PersistAsync stores the extractor's snippet and context text as given, and Fits checks lengths and offset order only (not Text == canonical[Start..End], not End within the text). With a real extractor, hostile syllabus text could get model-written words shown as a verbatim quote of the syllabus. The demotion opens no new route (Unclaimed is already extractor-supplied). Not reachable with the mock. | READ Jobs.cs:161-162,176-180; MockRuleExtractor.cs:21-22,490-497,505-507. SyllabusLimits.TryCanonical (MockRuleExtractor.cs:523-534) requires Fits, End and ContextEnd <= canonical.Length, all four offsets off a surrogate pair, and ordinal equality of Text and ContextText with the slices; it returns the server-cut slices, and PersistAsync (Jobs.cs:149-183) stores only those; forged snippets are counted and not demoted. (a) The weekly-rule reader deliberately skips the SBV-36b colon rule, so a colon time with no am/pm is read as 24-hour: 'Office hours Tuesdays 1:30-3:00' is a weekly rule at 01:30-03:00 and 'Office hours Tue/Thu 2:00-3:30' at 02:00-03:30, `probably_right` ('Class meets MWF 9:05-9:55' and '10:00-11:00' happen to be right). Kept OPEN rather than deferred because both are wrong times from ordinary text; LOW because (a) gives a visibly absurd small-hours office-hours slot, not a deadline, and (b) needs a written zone that differs from the course default the student chose. Personal-use calibration (PO-15/16). | RAN 2026-11-02 (scratch probe sbv35probe, stored values as quoted). READ MockRuleExtractor.cs:155, :163 (strictColon only for dated items), :204-214 (TryRecurrence uses `FindTime(line, null)`), :270 (timezone = course default). | SBV-39 | sbr-fixes-verification (twelfth pass) | LOW | OPEN | NONE | Introduced by the SBV-37 fix (9684247); reachable with the mock. All `probably_right` (with a written year `looks_right`). Before the fix these were evening starts with the end dropped, so this is not a regression in harm, only a different wrong time. Only identical start and end clocks reach 12 hours; any start earlier in the hour than the end gives under 12. Separately (pre-existing, not SBV-37): when am/pm is on the start, or there is none, an end that reads before the start is dropped without a flag ('11am-1' is 11:00 with no end, '11pm-1am' 23:00 with no end, '14:00-13:00' 14:00 with no end); the start is as written. Probe: 'Office hours Tuesdays by appointment', 'No class Mondays', and, since 9684247, 'Office hours Tuesdays 11-12am' and 'Office hours Tuesdays 11-12am and 3-4pm' (the readable second range is not tried, because `FindTime` returns no time at the first unorderable range rather than moving on) all give items=0, unclaimed=0, dropped=0. Before the fix the '11-12am' lines were weekly rules at 23:00 (wrong), so silence replaced a wrong time. Breaks the playbook rule 'every dropped item must be counted, not silent' and L-DROP. LOW, not MEDIUM: nothing lost is a dated deadline (dated lines always produce an item or a noticed date; '11-12am' on a dated line is all-day with `time_missing` on exams), nothing wrong is stored, and the student still has the syllabus; same class as SBV-33, which the Lead deferred as a mock limit, so the Lead may defer this one with the same reason. Fix: count such lines in `DroppedInvalid` (or add a needs-date item for an office-hours/no-class line with weekdays and no readable time), and in `FindTime` treat an unorderable range as 'no time here' only for that clock (continue) if the Lead wants the second range read. (d) was run without `--include-inherited`, so a Reader-plus-inherited assignment from a management group would not show; low residual for a personal subscription, not a closure condition. (e) not run (optional). These are the expected results. **What closed the row (done, PO session 2026-11-04, above):** the PO, in the PO's own terminal, runs (a), (b), (c) and (d) exactly as below **without running `az login` as the PO first** (the CLI already holds the tether-reader sign-in), sees the same four results, and records date, time and 'same results' (no ids, names or keys) on the Review Desk or in `docs/DECISIONS.md`. (e) stays optional. **New residual from (c), shared default profile:** agents and the PO use the same CLI profile folder, so if the PO ever runs `az login` as the Owner in any terminal, the Owner token lands where agents can reuse it and this finding reopens. Rule: the PO signs in as Owner only in the portal, or with a separate `AZURE_CONFIG_DIR` set for that terminal, followed by `az logout`. **Claimed fix (Lead, 2026-11-04, PO-20bf in `docs/DECISIONS.md`, READ):** owner login ended; service identity `tether-reader` created with only the built-in Reader role at **subscription** scope (the review asked for one resource group; Reader has no write and no listKeys action, so the wider scope is accepted as a residual, narrowing it is optional); Lead signed in as it and observed a test write (create resource group) refused with AuthorizationFailed and no role other than Reader; its secret is in `.tether-azure` in the PO's profile, outside every repo, expires in one year. **The read-only commands** (the PO runs them for closure, see above) in a terminal and records the dated output (metadata only; never commit subscription ids, tenant ids, app ids, resource names or keys to this public repo): (a) in the shell agents use, `az account show --query user.type -o tsv`, expect `servicePrincipal` (not `user`); (b) same shell, `az account list --all --query "[].user.type" -o tsv`, expect only `servicePrincipal` lines, no `user` line (no Owner session cached in that profile); (c) print `AZURE_CONFIG_DIR` (`echo $AZURE_CONFIG_DIR` in bash, `$env:AZURE_CONFIG_DIR` in PowerShell), expect either a path outside the repo (separate agent profile) or empty (agents share the default profile `%USERPROFILE%\.azure`); (e) optional, in the PO's own terminal only, never an agent's, signed in as tether-reader: `az cognitiveservices account keys list -n <resource> -g <group> -o none`, expect an AuthorizationFailed error naming `Microsoft.CognitiveServices/accounts/listKeys/action` (`-o none` means even an unexpected success prints no key). **Remaining parts (not closure conditions unless stated):** key regeneration is needed only if the Foundry keys could ever have been printed; The Reader identity lives in the default profile (`AZURE_CONFIG_DIR` unset, see the 2026-11-04 verification above), and (b) shows no `user` entry in it. | ID | Source | Sev | Status | Gate | Summary | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DXD-01 | docs-export-design-review 2.1 | MEDIUM | FIXED-VERIFIED | PRODUCTION | **Built-code review 2026-11-05 (HEAD 2100828), FIXED-VERIFIED:** `DocsExportService` takes (1) the session row FOR KEY SHARE through `IDocsSessionSource.LockAndReadAsync` on the caller's transaction, (2) the (user, session) advisory lock, (3) the user advisory lock, then rows doc_exports, idempotency_keys, demo_docs, doc_targets, audit_events; one transaction per attempt with a savepoint around the provider; deadlock and key-conflict repeat the whole attempt without raising `attempt`; only the three session FKs map 23503 to 404. Tests B23, B23a, B24, B25d (lecture delete, course purge, student delete; CommandTrap plus `pg_blocking_pids`; deadlock counter unchanged) READ and RAN 2026-11-05 in the SDK container: `Tether.DocsTests` 74 passed, `Tether.ArchitectureTests` 38 passed, `Tether.ContractTests --filter Docs` 76 passed, `Tether.IntegrationTests --filter DocsExport` 63 passed (dev API left running); web vitest docs/notes-export/delete suites 217 passed. Not proven by these tests: account erasure racing a writer (new row DXB-02). **Re-review 2026-11-05 (commit f7aa2ef), FIXED-CONTRACT-ONLY:** contract 4.3 and ADR section 5 now state one transaction per attempt (Q-A), the lock order session `FOR KEY SHARE` via a new Lectures.Contracts port on the caller's transaction, then `pg_advisory_xact_lock` (user, session), then docs rows; 23503 to 404, 23505 to replay, whole-attempt deadlock retry without raising `attempt`; pending/reaper/`export_in_progress` real-provider only; tasks B23, B23a, B24, B25c, B25d (CommandTrap, `pg_blocking_pids`, L-VICTIM). Residuals in new rows DXD-14 (the fixed docs-row order inserts `idempotency_keys` before the `doc_exports` row its new FK references) and DXD-15 (23505 replay without a fingerprint compare). Original finding: Design-stage. The write transaction model and lock order are unspecified and self-contradictory. 4.3 says one transaction (export row, provider call, document, target, audit), yet 4.4 and B23, B24 and B31 rely on a committed `pending` row, a 60 s reaper and a crash between provider write and record update, which cannot happen inside one transaction. The ADR's order ('session key-share via the FK, then lock doc_targets') does not say which statement comes first. A `SELECT ... FROM docs.doc_targets FOR UPDATE` before the first child insert takes doc_targets then session, the reverse of the cascade delete (session then children): exactly L-LOCKORDER. On the first `create` there is no doc_targets row to lock, so two confirms with different keys both proceed and the loser hits a PK `23505` (a 500 unless mapped). A lecture delete that commits first makes the writer's FK check fail with `23503` (must be 404, C-21). Student delete (Q3) and the reaper are writers too. Unsure between LOW and MEDIUM: L-LOCKORDER caused real CI failures and the outcome is intermittent 500s on the delete or the export, so MEDIUM. | READ ADR section 5 'Concurrency' and section 6; contract 4.3, 4.4; tasks B23, B24, B31; `SessionPorts.cs` (`ISessionSequencer` takes the caller's `DbTransaction`, `ISessionDirectory` does not); `SyllabusService.cs:186` (advisory lock pattern). Fix: one stated model (single transaction for the demo; pending/reaper labelled real-provider-only); first lock = session row `FOR KEY SHARE` via a new Lectures.Contracts port on the caller's transaction (null = 404), then `pg_advisory_xact_lock` on (user, session), then docs rows; reaper touches one table per transaction; map 23503 to 404 and the idempotency 23505 to replay; deadlock retry repeats the whole attempt without increasing `attempt`; CommandTrap + `pg_blocking_pids` tests for confirm against lecture delete, course purge and student delete, tolerant of losing the race (L-VICTIM). |

Inserting the key first violates that FK (unless it is DEFERRABLE INITIALLY DEFERRED), and the blanket rule '23503 -> 404' then turns every first confirm into a calm `404 not_found` instead of a visible bug. Lock-order effect: none (both rows are new in the same transaction), so this is a correctness and masking defect, not a deadlock. LOW: the first test (B13) fails, though it fails with a misleading 404. | READ contract 4.3 (step 3 order; 'Database errors: 23503 ... -> 404'), ADR section 5 (`docs.idempotency_keys` row, 'Persistence rules', 'Concurrency and lock order'). Fix: order `doc_exports` before `idempotency_keys` (or declare the FK deferrable and say so); map only `23503` raised by the named session FKs (`fk_*_session_user`) to 404 and let any other constraint surface as a 500 with a type-only log; B25c checks the constraint name. Owners Lead (text), backend-engineer. |
| DXD-15 | docs-export-design-review section 6 (re-review) | LOW | FIXED-VERIFIED | NONE | **Built-code review 2026-11-05 (HEAD 2100828), FIXED-VERIFIED:** a 23505 on `ux_docs_idempotency_keys_scope` repeats the whole attempt, whose key lookup compares fingerprints (equal: replay; different: 400 `reused`). Tests B25f (one key on two lectures at once; forced 23505) READ and RAN 2026-11-05 in the SDK container: `Tether.DocsTests` 74 passed, `Tether.ArchitectureTests` 38 passed, `Tether.ContractTests --filter Docs` 76 passed, `Tether.IntegrationTests --filter DocsExport` 63 passed (dev API left running); web vitest docs/notes-export/delete suites 217 passed. Residual of DXD-04. Contract 4.3 says '23505 on the idempotency key -> treated as a replay of the winner'. The key's unique scope is `(user, method, route template, key hash)`, the route template does not contain the session, and the advisory lock is per (user, session). So the same user sending the same key for two different lectures at the same time is not serialised; the loser hits 23505 and would 'replay' the winner's record, which belongs to the other lecture. Contract 4.4 itself says a different body (the fingerprint covers the session path) is `400`. Same user only, so there is no cross-user leak; the effect is a wrong record and a silent non-write. Syllabus compares the fingerprint on this path (`ReplayOrMismatchAsync`). | READ contract 4.3, 4.4, 4.1 step 5; `SyllabusService.cs:257-275`. Fix: on 23505, after the rollback read the winner and compare fingerprints in constant time: equal -> replay, different -> `400 validation_failed`; a test with one key on two sessions sent concurrently (CommandTrap). Owners Lead (text), backend-engineer. |
| DXD-16 | docs-export-design-review section 6 (re-review) | LOW | FIXED-VERIFIED | NONE | **Built-code review 2026-11-05 (HEAD 2100828), FIXED-VERIFIED:** every writer takes the per-user advisory lock after its session and (user, session) locks, and the cap is counted under it. Tests B25g (many lectures at once never pass a cap of 3; lecture delete in between neither deadlocks nor orphans) and the 200/201 cap test READ and RAN 2026-11-05 in the SDK container: `Tether.DocsTests` 74 passed, `Tether.ArchitectureTests` 38 passed, `Tether.ContractTests --filter Docs` 76 passed, `Tether.IntegrationTests --filter DocsExport` 63 passed (dev API left running); web vitest docs/notes-export/delete suites 217 passed. Residual of DXD-03. Contract 6 says the 200-documents-per-user cap 'is checked under the same advisory lock so it is race-free', and task B43a says the same, but that lock is on (user, session). Two confirms on different lectures of one user run concurrently, both count 199 and both write. Bounded overshoot (one per concurrent lecture), one user, mock: LOW. | READ contract 4.3 step (2), 6; tasks B43a. Fix: take a second advisory lock on the user alone, always after the session `FOR KEY SHARE` and before the (user, session) lock, in every writer (the cascade delete takes neither, so no cycle); or enforce the cap with a per-user counter row locked in that same position; B43a uses two different sessions concurrently. Owners Lead (text), backend-engineer. |
| DXD-17 | docs-export-design-review section 6 (re-review) | LOW | FIXED-VERIFIED | NONE | **Built-code review 2026-11-05 (HEAD 2100828), FIXED-VERIFIED:** a narrow Docs-only port `IDocsSessionSource`/`DocsSessionFacts`; `SessionInfo` is unchanged and has no Title. Test B02b (SessionInfo has no Title; Ai, Transcript, Syllabus and Courses never name the port) READ and RAN 2026-11-05 in the SDK container: `Tether.DocsTests` 74 passed, `Tether.ArchitectureTests` 38 passed, `Tether.ContractTests --filter Docs` 76 passed, `Tether.IntegrationTests --filter DocsExport` 63 passed (dev API left running); web vitest docs/notes-export/delete suites 217 passed. Introduced by the Q-B decision (DXD-08 fix). The additive `SessionInfo.Title` is returned by `ISessionDirectory.FindAsync`, which the Ai module already calls in `AiGateway`, `NotesService`, `CatchUpService` and `SummaryEngine`. The student-typed lecture title therefore becomes available next to the vendor call, where nothing uses it today. No leak now (mock client, no code reads it); the risk is quiet drift: a later prompt change could send the title to a real model without a review. Data minimisation. | RAN grep `ISessionDirectory` over `src/api/src/Modules` outside Lectures (hits in `AiGateway.cs:111`, `NotesService.cs:131`, `CatchUpService.cs:101`, `SummaryEngine.cs:259`, `AiEndpoints.cs` and Transcript). READ ADR section 4 (Q-B). Fix: either a separate narrow port used only by Docs (for example `ISessionTitleReader`), or an architecture or source-scan test that `Tether.Ai` never reads `SessionInfo.Title`; any future use in a prompt goes through security and ai-agent review. Owners Lead, backend-engineer. |
| DXB-01 | docs-export-design-review section 7 (built code) | LOW | OPEN | NONE | Markdown notes export (item 2): `mdText` escapes emphasis, code, brackets, angle brackets, headings, tables, entities and list markers, but not bare URLs, `www.` hosts or e-mail addresses. GitHub-flavoured Markdown viewers (GitHub, VS Code preview, Obsidian and others) auto-link those, so a URL in AI notes text (for example injected through a lecture or, later, a real model) is a clickable link in the student's `.md` file. The doc comment says the text 'can not open a link', which overclaims; the test `odd characters in titles and AI text stay text` expects `http://e/x.png` to pass through unchanged. The URL is shown as itself (no disguised link text), the file is the student's own, and the mock writes no URLs: LOW. The Demo Doc page is not affected (plain React text nodes, no linkifier). | READ `src/web/src/components/lecture/notesExport.ts:41-51`, `notesExport.test.ts:136-151`. RAN vitest (217 passed). Not run: any Markdown renderer (none is installed in `src/web`), so GFM behaviour is from the GFM spec's autolink extension, UNVERIFIED in a specific viewer. Fix: break autolink literals (for example backslash-escape the `:` of `scheme://`, and the `.` after `www` and before the domain of an `@` address), verified with a GFM renderer such as micromark with the gfm-autolink-literal extension in a test, and correct the doc comment. Owner frontend-engineer. |
| DXB-02 | docs-export-design-review section 7 (built code) | LOW | OPEN | NONE | Account erasure versus a running Docs writer (reasoned, not reproduced). Confirm and student delete hold the session row FOR KEY SHARE from their first statement and take the `identity.users` row (FOR KEY SHARE, via `fk_docs_audit_events_user`) only at the last insert, the audit row. A cascade from `delete from identity.users` (the erasure path PO-20aw names; B05b uses it) locks the user row first, then must delete the session row. The two orders form a cycle, so PostgreSQL aborts one side (40P01). The Docs side is safe (`WithRetryAsync` repeats, then answers 404 once the lecture is gone). The erasure side fails unless its caller retries, and no erasure caller exists yet (NEW-01). L-LOCKORDER class; possibly present wherever a writer inserts a row with an `identity.users` FK after locking a session or course row (Syllabus has `fk_idempotency_keys_user`); not swept. LOW: no erasure API, no data loss, detected deadlock. | READ `DocsExportService.cs:137-150,266-272,305-311`, D001 `fk_docs_audit_events_user`, `M006_LecturesIntegrity.cs:28` (`fk_lecture_sessions_user_id` CASCADE), `DocsExportPersistenceTests.B05b` (no concurrency). Not run: no reproduction (would need a CommandTrap test with a raw erasure connection). Fix, when NEW-01's erasure is built: the erasure job repeats the whole delete on 40P01/40001 (L-VICTIM) and gets a CommandTrap race test against a confirm parked after its session lock; alternatively writers take the user row KEY SHARE before the session row (a cross-module port, more invasive). Owners backend-engineer, database-architect. |
